# Disk transfer representations and confined bridge mass

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-M-0481` | `PM-237.8` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Disk-transfer-representations-and-confined-bridge-mass-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Disk-transfer-representations-and-confined-bridge-mass-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that critical honeycomb bridges crossing a strip of width <i>R</i> have mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$, with the same exponent after confinement to a fixed multiple of the strip width. The initial boundary port is fixed, the terminal port is summed, and all finite lengths receive their critical weights. The bridge mass is comparable to <i>R</i><sup>−1/4</sup>; the half-plane arch kernel at endpoint separation <i>m</i> is comparable to <i>m</i><sup>−5/4</sup>.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that critical honeycomb bridges crossing a strip of width <i>R</i> have mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$, with the same exponent after confinement to a fixed multiple of the strip width. The initial boundary port is fixed, the terminal port is summed, and all finite lengths receive their critical weights. The bridge mass is comparable to <i>R</i><sup>−1/4</sup>; the half-plane arch kernel at endpoint separation <i>m</i> is comparable to <i>m</i><sup>−5/4</sup>.

- **claim-fixed-corridor-bridge-mean** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-confined-bridge)): There is a constant $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every fixed $C\ge C_0$, $$B_C(R)\asymp B(R)\asymp R^{-1/4},\qquad M_C(R)=R^{13/12+o(1)},\qquad M(R)=R^{13/12+o(1)}.$$ Consequently the mean number of visited vertices is $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ both under the unrestricted critical bridge law and under its restriction to this fixed corridor.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently wide fixed corridor retains a comparable bridge mass and the same first-moment exponent as the unrestricted strip. Normalizing each first moment by its own bridge mass gives the common mean-length exponent. Length counts visited vertices, and the terminal port is summed.
- **claim-boundary-and-annealed-chord-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c2-thm-annealed)): The following conclusions hold as the indicated scale tends to infinity.* 1. *The arch kernel is nonincreasing and $$h(m)\asymp m^{-5/4},\qquad \mu(\operatorname{height}>R)\asymp \mu(\operatorname{diameter}>R)\asymp R^{-1/4}.$$* 2. *For every sufficiently large fixed $C$, normalize $\mu$ either on $R\le\operatorname{diameter}\le CR$, or on $\operatorname{height}\ge R$ and $\operatorname{diameter}\le CR$. Under either law, $\mathbb E\ell=R^{4/3+o(1)}$.* 3. *Let $$D_R=\{y:e_i\cdot y<R,\ i=0,1,2\},\qquad I_R=\{v\text{ a lattice vertex}:e_i\cdot v<(1-\varepsilon)R, \ i=0,1,2\},$$ where $R\in\frac12+\frac32\mathbb Z$ and $\varepsilon>0$ is sufficiently small and fixed. Sum critical chord weights over the ordered pair of boundary ports and normalize on chords visiting $I_R$. Uniformly for $v\in I_R$, their visit probability is $R^{-2/3+o(1)}$. The expected total number of visited vertices, and the expected number in $I_R$, are both $R^{4/3+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: The theorem gives a decreasing boundary kernel and matching height and diameter tail orders. It also determines mean length for macroscopic arches and bulk visit probabilities for triangle chords conditioned to visit the inner triangle. The endpoint summations and the conditioning events are part of these conclusions.
- **claim-half-cylinder-inverse-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-inverse)): For $N\ge3$ and generic distinct rapidities sufficiently near zero, define $$\mathcal C_{ij}=\frac1{x_i+q x_j}\qquad(1\le i,j\le N).$$ Then $$\sum_i x_i-c\sum_{i\ne j}e^{\mathrm i(\eta_i+\eta_j)} H_{ij}(\boldsymbol\eta) ={\bf1}^{\mathsf T}(\mathcal C^{\mathsf T}+q\mathcal C)^{-1}{\bf1}. \label{c2:eq-9}$$ The scalar pairing on the right has a removable continuation to a neighborhood of homogeneous rapidities. This does not assert that the matrix inverse itself extends to coincident rapidities.
  - Explanation: The endpoint exchange equations determine the summed arc deficit through a finite Cauchy-matrix inverse pairing. The scalar pairing has a removable homogeneous continuation; the proposition explicitly does not extend the matrix inverse itself through rapidity collisions.
- **claim-polynomial-disk-transfer-vector** (construction, [source](#stmt-c2-prop-disk-vector)): 1. *For each disk matching $\alpha$, let $a_i\in\{0,1\}$ record whether its site $i$ is occupied. Its coordinate satisfies $$\Phi_{N,\alpha}=\left(\prod_i x_i^{-a_i/2}\right)p_N(x)\psi_{N,\alpha} \quad\text{is polynomial, of degree }\le N-1-a_i\text{ in }x_i . \label{c2:eq-23}$$ This prescription is for $N\ge1$; we suppress the coordinate subscript when the matching is fixed.* 2. *Exchange of adjacent labels is implemented by $\check R$ as above. At adjacent pairs with earlier rapidity equal to later rapidity plus $3\lambda,2\lambda$, respectively, $\psi_N$ reduces to $K\psi_{N-2},J\psi_{N-1}$ inserted at that pair; the new single label for $J$ has midpoint rapidity. Here these are identities for generic parameters subject to the indicated condition, with disk rotation allowed (we call these the descending pairs). The row fixes $\psi_N$. With one variable tending to zero or infinity and the rest generic, the normalized vector tends to the one with that site vacant and deleted.
  - Explanation: Clearing the empty-normalized disk vector by the scalar polynomial and the occupancy gauge produces polynomial coordinates with sitewise degree bounds. The same construction supplies adjacent exchanges, descending fusion reductions, transfer invariance and deletion limits.
- **claim-scalar-pairing-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c2-lem-scalar-uniqueness)): Suppose $V_{k,l}(X_A;X_B)$, for $k,l\ge0$, are separately symmetric rational functions of the two variable lists. Assume that $p_N(X)^2V_{k,l}(X_A;X_B)$ is a polynomial of degree at most $2N-2$ in each variable for $N\ge1$, and that the deletion, merging, single-site and two-site scalar rules above hold. Then the family is determined by $V_{0,0}$ and $V_{1,1}$.
  - Explanation: Separate symmetry, the polynomial degree bound and all the stated scalar reduction rules determine the entire pairing family from its specified base values. The cross-block cluster rule removes the residual ambiguity at balanced sizes.
- **claim-neutral-scalar-gas-identity** (construction, [source](#stmt-c2-prop-scalar-gas)): For the two consecutive blocks and the factors just defined, $$\mathcal P_N^2 V_{k,l}= \sum_{\sum_A r_i=\sum_B r_i=j} e^{i\theta j}\mu^{\sum_i \|r_i\|} \prod_{i<h}g_{t(i,h),r_i,r_h}(z_h-z_i), \label{c2:eq-24}$$ where $j$ also ranges over the integers.
  - Explanation: The centered scalar polynomial squared times the twisted disk pairing equals a finite gas sum with signed neutrality. The gas labels are distinct from the spin coordinates; equality follows by checking the scalar uniqueness conditions.
- **claim-homogeneous-gas-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-c2-thm-contour-bound)): As $n\to\infty$, $$\log\|\mathcal Z_n(\theta)e^{-A_\infty n^2}\|/\log n \ \le\ \tfrac38-\tfrac{2}{3\pi^2}\Re(\theta^2)+o(1),\qquad A_\infty=\log\!\big(4(2+d)^2/81\big) \label{c2:eq-V1}$$ uniformly in a fixed complex neighborhood of $[0,\pi/2]$.
  - Explanation: After removing its exponential scalar factor, the homogeneous gas has a locally uniform logarithmic upper bound near the real angle interval. Current reflection, residue stability and the nome limit establish the estimate for complex angles.
- **claim-homogeneous-pfaffian-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c2-lem-pfaffian-normalization)): For $m=2n$, $$P_m^2=[(2+d)2/3]^{m(m-1)}(2/3)^m\, n^{5/24+o(1)}. \label{c2:eq-V8}$$
  - Explanation: The homogeneous even-size scalar polynomial has the stated exponential normalization and logarithmic power correction. Its normalization is obtained independently of the contour bound from a relative moment determinant.
- **claim-harmonic-upper-bound-saturation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-c2-lem-saturation)): Let $b>0$, let $\Omega\subset\mathbb C$ be an open neighborhood of $[0,b]$, and let $F_n$ be holomorphic on $\Omega$. Suppose $F_n(t)>0$ and $F_n(t)\le F_n(0)$ for $0\le t\le b$. Let $h$ be harmonic on $\Omega$, and suppose, locally uniformly there, $$\frac{\log\|F_n(z)\|}{\log n}\le h(z)+o(1),\qquad \frac{\log F_n(b)}{\log n}\mathrel{\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}\longrightarrow\pdfliteral page{EMC}}h(b).$$ Then $\log F_n(0)/\log n\mathrel{\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}\longrightarrow\pdfliteral page{EMC}}h(0)$.
  - Explanation: Positivity and domination by the value at the initial endpoint, together with a locally uniform harmonic upper bound and equality at the other endpoint, force equality at the initial endpoint. Subharmonic barriers propagate any proposed deficit to contradict the known endpoint equality.
- **claim-two-gap-cylinder-exponent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c2-prop-cylinder-exponent)): On the unit-edge honeycomb cylinder of period $2nU$, $n\ge3$, choose the seam face centers $f$ and $f+nU$ from the gluing construction in Section <a href="#sec:pd:physical-pairing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pd:physical-pairing">5.3.0.1</a>. Let $V_{n,n}(\theta)$ sum collections of vertex-disjoint finite simple polygons separating these marks, with weight $x^{\|\gamma\|}(2\cos\theta)$ per polygon. Polygons are unrooted and unoriented, $\|\gamma\|$ counts their vertices, and the empty collection has weight one. At angle zero, equivalently at polygon fugacity two, $$V_{n,n}(0)=n^{1/6+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: At balanced homogeneity the twisted disk pairing is the positive partition sum of separating polygons. Combining its complex-angle upper bound, independent scalar normalization and exact zero-fugacity value gives the angle-zero separator exponent.
- **claim-adjacent-bridges-and-polygon-shapes** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-adjacent-bridges-shapes)): For an admissible strip of height $R$, let $F(R)=f_{1,1}$ be the critical mass of pairs of vertex-disjoint bridges with adjacent endpoints on each wall, fixing the first bottom port and summing the first top port. For a full-plane simple polygon $\gamma$, let $[\gamma]$ denote its unrooted, unoriented translation class under the lattice generated by $U,V$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$F(R)\le CR^{-3},\qquad \sum_{\substack{[\gamma]\\L\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\gamma<2L}}x^{\|\gamma\|} \le CL^{-2}\qquad(L\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: Moving vacancies among ordered bridges yields the adjacent-endpoint pair bound. An injective extreme-row cutting construction converts each polygon translation class into such a pair and gives the dyadic polygon-shape mass bound.
- **claim-uniform-winding-separator-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-winding-separators)): On the honeycomb cylinder of period $2nU$, $n\ge3$, let $f$ be a point off the lattice edges and put $f'=f+nU$. Then $$\sum_{\substack{\eta\ {\rm winding\ on\ full\ cylinder}\\ \eta\ {\rm separates}\ f,f'}} x^{\|\eta\|} \le 2/t_0. \label{c2:eq-C3}$$ The polygons in this sum are simple, unrooted and unoriented.
  - Explanation: A finite winding-polygon matrix has spectral radius below the critical threshold by exploration and continuous layer addition. Opposite side assignments of the antipodal marks then bound the total winding separator mass uniformly before vertical exhaustion.
- **claim-finite-visit-recursion** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-visits)): For every finite filled $D$ and $v\in D$, $$\begin{aligned} T(v,D)=3 &+\sum_{\substack{\Gamma\subset D\\v\in\Gamma}} 2x^{\|\Gamma\|-1}\big[-\cos\big(s(2\pi-\tau_v(\Gamma))\big)\big] \notag\\ &+\sum_{\substack{\Gamma\subset D\\v\in\Gamma^\circ}} 2x^{\|\Gamma\|}T(v,\Gamma^\circ). \label{c2:eq-G2} \end{aligned}$$ Every coefficient on the right is positive. In particular, $T(v,D)\ge3$ and $T(v,D)$ is monotone under inclusion of finite filled sets containing $v$. There are absolute positive constants such that $$T(v,D)\asymp\sum_{f\text{ incident to }v}Z(D;f). \label{c2:eq-G3}$$ If $D$ is the vertex set inside a bounded convex polygon cut out by admissible walls, and $A_D(v)$ denotes the corresponding positive, ordered excursion weight through $v$, then $$\cos(3\pi/8)A_D(v)\le T(v,D)\le A_D(v). \label{eq:positive-visits}$$
  - Explanation: Finite nonbacktracking exploration gives a positive polygon recursion for the real visit sum. Unrolling the recursion compares it with incident-face nesting sums and proves filled-domain monotonicity. Comparison with positive excursion mass additionally requires an admissible convex domain.
- **claim-fixed-end-straight-tube-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pd-tube)): There is an absolute $\delta>0$ with the following property. For every fixed $\eta>0$ there are $c_\eta>0$ and $L_\eta<\infty$ such that, for admissible strip width $L\ge L_\eta$ and prescribed initial and terminal ports with lateral offset at most $\delta L$, the total critical bridge mass within lateral distance $\eta L$ of their linear interpolation is at least $c_\eta L^{-1-p}$, where $p=1/4$.
  - Explanation: For sufficiently large admissible widths, small prescribed lateral offsets admit critical bridges inside an arbitrarily narrow fixed-proportion straight tube with the stated fixed-end mass lower bound. The proof controls repeated representations through a weighted second moment.

```json
        "anchor": "stmt-c2-lem-saturation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b>0$, let $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb C$ be an open neighborhood of $[0,b]$, and let $F_n$ be holomorphic on $\\Omega$. Let $h$ be harmonic on $\\Omega$, and suppose, locally uniformly there, $$\\frac{\\log|F_n(z)|}{\\log n}\\le h(z)+o(1),\\qquad\n \\frac{\\log F_n(b)}{\\log n}\\mathrel{\\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}\\longrightarrow\\pdfliteral page{EMC}}h(b).$$ Then $\\log F_n(0)/\\log n\\mathrel{\\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}\\longrightarrow\\pdfliteral page{EMC}}h(0)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.8"
      },

Let $h$ be harmonic on $\\Omega$, and suppose, locally uniformly there, $$\\frac{\\log|F_n(z)|}{\\log n}\\le h(z)+o(1),\\qquad\n \\frac{\\log F_n(b)}{\\log n}\\mathrel{\\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}\\longrightarrow\\pdfliteral page{EMC}}h(b).$$ Then $\\log F_n(0)/\\log n\\mathrel{\\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}\\longrightarrow\\pdfliteral page{EMC}}h(0)$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-c2-prop-cylinder-exponent",
        "exact_quote": "On the unit-edge honeycomb cylinder of period $2nU$, $n\\ge3$, choose the seam face centers $f$ and $f+nU$ from the gluing construction in Section <a href=\"#sec:pd:physical-pairing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:pd:physical-pairing\">5.3.0.1</a>. Let $V_{n,n}(\\theta)$ sum collections of vertex-disjoint finite simple polygons separating these marks, with weight $x^{|\\gamma|}(2\\cos\\theta)$ per polygon. Polygons are unrooted and unoriented, $|\\gamma|$ counts their vertices, and the empty collection has weight one. At angle zero, equivalently at polygon fugacity two, $$V_{n,n}(0)=n^{1/6+o(1)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.8"
      },
      "evidence": [

Polygons are unrooted and unoriented, $|\\gamma|$ counts their vertices, and the empty collection has weight one. At angle zero, equivalently at polygon fugacity two, $$V_{n,n}(0)=n^{1/6+o(1)}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-adjacent-bridges-shapes",
        "exact_quote": "For an admissible strip of height $R$, let $F(R)=f_{1,1}$ be the critical mass of pairs of vertex-disjoint bridges with adjacent endpoints on each wall, fixing the first bottom port and summing the first top port. For a full-plane simple polygon $\\gamma$, let $[\\gamma]$ denote its unrooted, unoriented translation class under the lattice generated by $U,V$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$F(R)\\le CR^{-3},\\qquad\n \\sum_{\\substack{[\\gamma]\\\\L\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\gamma<2L}}x^{|\\gamma|}\n \\le CL^{-2}\\qquad(L\\ge1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.8"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-visits",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite filled $D$ and $v\\in D$, $$\\begin{aligned}\nT(v,D)=3\n &+\\sum_{\\substack{\\Gamma\\subset D\\\\v\\in\\Gamma}}\n       2x^{|\\Gamma|-1}\\big[-\\cos\\big(s(2\\pi-\\tau_v(\\Gamma))\\big)\\big]\n \\notag\\\\\n &+\\sum_{\\substack{\\Gamma\\subset D\\\\v\\in\\Gamma^\\circ}}\n       2x^{|\\Gamma|}T(v,\\Gamma^\\circ).                 \\label{c2:eq-G2}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every coefficient on the right is positive. In particular, $T(v,D)\\ge3$ and $T(v,D)$ is monotone under inclusion of finite filled sets containing $v$. There are absolute positive constants such that $$T(v,D)\\asymp\\sum_{f\\text{ incident to }v}Z(D;f).       \\label{c2:eq-G3}$$ If $D$ is the vertex set inside a bounded convex polygon cut out by admissible walls, and $A_D(v)$ denotes the corresponding positive, ordered excursion weight through $v$, then $$\\cos(3\\pi/8)A_D(v)\\le T(v,D)\\le A_D(v).              \\label{eq:positive-visits}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-visits",
          "exact_quote": "For every finite filled $D$ and $v\\in D$, $$\\begin{aligned}\nT(v,D)=3\n &+\\sum_{\\substack{\\Gamma\\subset D\\\\v\\in\\Gamma}}\n       2x^{|\\Gamma|-1}\\big[-\\cos\\big(s(2\\pi-\\tau_v(\\Gamma))\\big)\\big]\n \\notag\\\\\n &+\\sum_{\\substack{\\Gamma\\subset D\\\\v\\in\\Gamma^\\circ}}\n       2x^{|\\Gamma|}T(v,\\Gamma^\\circ).                 \\label{c2:eq-G2}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every coefficient on the right is positive. In particular, $T(v,D)\\ge3$ and $T(v,D)$ is monotone under inclusion of finite filled sets containing $v$. There are absolute positive constants such that $$T(v,D)\\asymp\\sum_{f\\text{ incident to }v}Z(D;f).       \\label{c2:eq-G3}$$ If $D$ is the vertex set inside a bounded convex polygon cut out by admissible walls, and $A_D(v)$ denotes the corresponding positive, ordered excursion weight through $v$, then $$\\cos(3\\pi/8)A_D(v)\\le T(v,D)\\le A_D(v).              \\label{eq:positive-visits}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.8"
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible wall** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $i\in\{0,1,2\}$ and $t\in\frac12+\frac32\mathbb Z$, the line $e_i\cdot y=t$ is an admissible wall.
- **Boundary ports** ([source](#pm-h-3)): These midpoints are boundary *ports*.
- **Port-to-port path** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A port-to-port path visits distinct lattice vertices, all strictly inside its prescribed domain.
- **Critical vertex weight** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Throughout, a path $\gamma$ with $\ell(\gamma)$ visited vertices has weight $$w(\gamma)=x^{\ell(\gamma)},\qquad x=(2\cos(\pi/8))^{-1}=(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}.$$
- **Asymptotic comparison notation** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We write $f(R)\asymp g(R)$ when their ratio stays between positive constants, and $f(R)=R^{a+o(1)}$ when $\log f(R)/\log R\to a$.
- **Bridge** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *bridge* joins $a$ to any port of the upper wall and otherwise stays strictly between the walls.
- **Bridge mass and raw first length moment** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $$B(R)=\sum_{\gamma\text{ bridge}}w(\gamma),\qquad M(R)=\sum_{\gamma\text{ bridge}}w(\gamma)\ell(\gamma).$$
- **Normalized critical bridge law** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The normalized critical bridge law assigns probability $w(\gamma)/B(R)$.
- **Fixed-corridor bridge sums** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $C>0$, let $B_C(R)$ and $M_C(R)$ be the same sums restricted to paths satisfying $\sup_{y\in\gamma}\|\xi(y)\|\le CR$, where the supremum includes the terminal ports.
- **Half-plane arch and endpoint kernel** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A half-plane *arch* starts at one fixed port and ends at another port of the same wall. Let $h(m)$ be its total critical mass when the terminal port lies $m>0$ port spacings to the right.
- **Unnormalized full-wall arch measure** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $\mu$ be the unnormalized critical measure with that initial port and the terminal port summed over the wall.
- **Multipath** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A multipath is a collection of paths with disjoint vertices and distinct endpoints.
- **Disk matching space** ([source](#c2:sec-disk-states)): Let $\mathcal D_N$ be the vector space with basis the partial noncrossing pairings of $N$ cyclically ordered boundary points of a disk.
- **Symmetric scalar Pfaffian polynomial** ([source](#c2:eq-22)): Define the symmetric polynomial $$p_N(x)=\operatorname{Pf}(H)\prod_{i<j}\frac{d_{ij}}{x_j-x_i},\qquad d_{ij}=x_i^2+x_j^2+d x_i x_j,\quad d=\sqrt2,\quad H_{ij}=\frac{x_j^2-x_i^2}{d_{ij}},\qquad p_0=p_1=1 .$$
- **Twisted disk pairing** ([source](#c2:sec-scalar-gas)): For $\theta\in\mathbb C$, set $$V_{k,l}=\psi_N(-z)^t m^{\sum_{i\in A}s_i}\psi_N(z),\qquad m=q^{-2} e^{i\theta},$$
- **Planar nesting partition function** ([source](#c2:nesting)): For a lattice face center $f$ in the plane let $Z_R(f)$ be the sum over collections of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple polygons all enclosing $f$, with their vertices in the Euclidean ball of radius $R$ about $f$.
- **Filled finite vertex set** ([source](#sec:finite-visits)): A finite vertex set $D$ is *filled* if every lattice vertex strictly inside a simple polygon in its induced graph also belongs to $D$.
- **Spin-weighted bulk visit sum** ([source](#sec:finite-visits)): For $v\in D$, define $$T(v,D)=\sum_{\substack{\gamma\text{ excursion in }D\\v\in\gamma}} x^{\ell(\gamma)}e^{isW(\gamma)},\qquad s=3/8,$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-vertex-activity | Critical activity per visited vertex | = | $(2\cos(\pi/8))^{-1}=(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}$ | The critical activity is fixed throughout the positive path measures. |
| q-parafermionic-spin | Parafermionic spin | = | $3/8$ | Fixed spin used in winding phases. |
| q-confined-first-length-moment | Confined bridge raw first length moment | = | $R^{13/12+o(1)}$ | Strip width tends to infinity with the corridor multiplier fixed. |
| q-unrestricted-first-length-moment | Unrestricted bridge raw first length moment | = | $R^{13/12+o(1)}$ | Admissible strip width tends to infinity. |
| q-bridge-mean-length | Mean visited-vertex count under either bridge law | = | $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ | The corridor multiplier is fixed while the strip width tends to infinity. |
| q-macroscopic-arch-mean | Mean length of conditioned macroscopic arches | = | $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ | The scale tends to infinity with a sufficiently large fixed diameter multiplier. |
| q-triangle-bulk-visit-probability | Visit probability of an inner-triangle vertex | = | $R^{-2/3+o(1)}$ | The triangle scale tends to infinity; the inner-triangle fraction is fixed. |
| q-triangle-expected-visits | Expected total and inner-triangle visited-vertex counts | = | $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ | Admissible triangle scale tends to infinity. |
| q-homogeneous-gas-logarithmic-upper-bound | Logarithmic growth of the gas after exponential normalization | ≤ | $\tfrac38-\tfrac{2}{3\pi^2}\Re(\theta^2)+o(1)$ | Locally uniform complex-angle estimate as the cylinder scale tends to infinity. |
| q-infinite-period-scalar-factor | Infinite-period exponential scalar coefficient | = | $\log\!\big(4(2+d)^2/81\big)$ | The scalar parameter is the square root specified in the disk construction. |
| q-even-pfaffian-homogeneous-square | Squared homogeneous even-size Pfaffian polynomial | = | $[(2+d)2/3]^{m(m-1)}(2/3)^m\, n^{5/24+o(1)}$ | Even size tends to infinity through the stated size relation. |
| q-cylinder-separator-partition | Positive two-gap cylinder separator partition function | = | $n^{1/6+o(1)}$ | Balanced cylinder scale tends to infinity. |
| q-adjacent-bridge-pair-upper-bound | Adjacent-endpoint disjoint bridge-pair mass | ≤ | $CR^{-3}$ | Absolute constant independent of admissible strip height. |
| q-polygon-translation-class-upper-bound | Dyadic polygon translation-class critical mass | ≤ | $CL^{-2}$ | Diameter scale satisfies the lower bound in the proposition. |
| q-winding-separator-uniform-upper-bound | Total single-polygon winding separator mass | ≤ | $2/t_0$ | Uniform over the periods and marks covered by the statement. |
| q-planar-nesting-partition | Planar face-center nesting partition function | = | $R^{z+o(1)}$ | The enclosing ball radius tends to infinity. |
| q-planar-nesting-exponent | Planar nesting exponent | = | $\frac1{12}$ | Asymptotic exponent of the positive nesting partition sum. |
| q-filled-domain-visit-lower-bound | Real spin-weighted visit sum | ≥ | $3$ | All finite domains and marked vertices covered by the proposition. |
| q-convex-domain-positive-visit-lower-comparison | Real visit sum compared with positive ordered excursion mass | ≥ | $\cos(3\pi/8)A_D(v)$ | The comparison is uniform with the fixed winding-spin normalization. |
| q-convex-domain-positive-visit-upper-comparison | Real visit sum compared with positive ordered excursion mass | ≤ | $A_D(v)$ | The same domain condition as the lower comparison. |
| q-straight-tube-fixed-end-lower-bound | Fixed-end bridge mass inside the straight tube | ≥ | $c_\eta L^{-1-p}$ | Tube proportion is fixed; width exceeds its associated threshold. |

```json
          "exact_quote": "On the unit-edge honeycomb cylinder of period $2nU$, $n\\ge3$, choose the seam face centers $f$ and $f+nU$ from the gluing construction in Section <a href=\"#sec:pd:physical-pairing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:pd:physical-pairing\">5.3.0.1</a>.         }

          "exact_quote": "For an admissible strip of height $R$, let $F(R)=f_{1,1}$ be the critical mass of pairs of vertex-disjoint bridges with adjacent endpoints on each wall, fixing the first bottom port and summing the first top port. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
```
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-boundary-exploration-and-physical-vacuum** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-inverse) / [proof](#pm-h-10): Finite exploration cancellation bounds multipath coordinates; accessibility then proves convergence of the nonempty transfer block and identifies the analytic fixed vector with physical arc masses. -> Unique analytic empty-normalized half-cylinder vector and endpoint exchange equations.
- **step-rapidity-orbit-inverse-pairing** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-inverse) / [proof](#pm-h-20): Reduce endpoint transports to a one-coordinate spectral problem, establish the scalar commutant, telescope arc sums and evaluate the resulting scalar with Cauchy resolvents. -> Inverse pairing and removable continuation of its scalar value.
- **step-sharp-boundary-mass** [claim](#stmt-c2-thm-annealed) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Take the confluent inverse pairing, compare its moment weight with a positive one and estimate the polynomial evaluation norm. -> Sharp cylinder boundary deficit.
- **step-height-strip-and-endpoint-kernel** [claim](#stmt-c2-thm-annealed) / [proof](#pm-h-28): Compare lost cylinder arcs with planar excursions, use wedge identities and averaged absorption for height and endpoint tails, then prove kernel monotonicity by continuation and switching. -> Sharp arch kernel, height and diameter tails, and strip bridge mass comparison.
- **step-disk-vector-fusion-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-c2-prop-disk-vector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c2-prop-disk-vector): Prescribe fused values, prove compatibility at intersecting hyperplanes, interpolate occupied coordinates independently of the chosen occupied site, then verify exchange and transfer invariance. -> Polynomial disk transfer coordinates, degree bounds, fusion reductions and deletion limits.
- **step-scalar-family-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-c2-lem-scalar-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c2-lem-scalar-uniqueness): Choose the least differing size, count specialization roots after clearing denominators and use the cross-block cluster limit to eliminate the balanced residual constant. -> Uniqueness from the stated scalar base values.
- **step-explicit-neutral-scalar-gas** [claim](#stmt-c2-prop-scalar-gas) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c2-prop-scalar-gas): Check apparent-pole cancellation, fusion constants and neutral cluster limits in the finite label sum, then apply scalar uniqueness. -> Exact finite scalar-gas representation.
- **step-contour-residues-and-current-reflection** [claim](#stmt-c2-thm-contour-bound) / [proof](#pm-h-52): Encode occupied gas sites by residues, periodize the integrands, realize the gas as an oscillator trace and conjugate the Weyl action to reflect the currents. -> Inverse interaction, decaying reflected source, scalar factor and momentum Gaussian.
- **step-residue-stability-and-gas-bound** [claim](#stmt-c2-thm-contour-bound) / [proof](#pm-h-74): Classify bound residue particles, separate the integrated positive cross-label term, retain coercive and electric energies, smear complete compounds, sum particle orders and continue to nome zero. -> Uniform complex-angle homogeneous gas upper bound.
- **step-independent-pfaffian-normalization** [claim](#stmt-c2-lem-pfaffian-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c2-lem-pfaffian-normalization): Convert the confluent Pfaffian square to a relative moment determinant, derive projection trace and leakage estimates, and integrate the logarithmic derivative of an accretive multiplier. -> Homogeneous scalar normalization with its logarithmic correction.
- **step-harmonic-saturation-principle** [claim](#stmt-c2-lem-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c2-lem-saturation): Assume a persistent deficit at the initial endpoint and use subharmonic rectangular barriers to propagate it to the endpoint with known equality. -> Equality of the logarithmic exponent at the initial endpoint.
- **step-cylinder-separator-exponent** [claim](#stmt-c2-prop-cylinder-exponent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c2-prop-cylinder-exponent): Use exact physical gluing to cancel exponential factors, subtract the scalar normalization correction and apply harmonic saturation at the exact zero-fugacity value. -> Angle-zero two-gap cylinder partition exponent.
- **step-adjacent-bridge-and-shape-bounds** [claim](#stmt-prop-adjacent-bridges-shapes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-adjacent-bridges-shapes): Move vacancies among ordered bridges, obtain positive interval-summed arch-loss bounds, telescope to impose adjacency at both walls and cut polygon extremes injectively. -> Adjacent bridge-pair and polygon translation-class bounds.
- **step-winding-separator-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-winding-separators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-winding-separators): Construct a nonnegative winding-polygon matrix, exclude unit spectral radius by exploration, add layers continuously and compare the antipodal separator classes before exhaustion. -> Uniform full-cylinder winding separator mass bound.
- **step-cylinder-to-planar-nesting** [claim](#stmt-c2-prop-cylinder-exponent) / [proof](#eq:disk-localization): Inject independent small nesting collections around the cylinder marks and bound all large contractible and winding separators by a bounded partition factor. -> Planar face-center nesting exponent.
- **step-finite-nesting-to-visit-recursion** [claim](#stmt-prop-visits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-visits): Compare boundary first-arrival prefixes with explorations from the marked vertex, classify return and repeat polygons and unroll the positive recursion into nested chains. -> Exact visit recursion, positivity, monotonicity and incident-face comparison.
- **step-triangle-and-arch-annealed-laws** [claim](#stmt-c2-thm-annealed) / [proof](#pm-h-96): Combine nesting and convex positive-visit comparison for triangle chords; sew an exterior arm to triangle excursions and control recoverable representations by weighted first and second moments. -> Triangle bulk visit law and conditioned macroscopic arch mean.
- **step-separated-chord-endpoint-supply** [claim](#stmt-thm-confined-bridge) / [proof](#pm-h-107): Derive the marked three-leg identity, control intersecting chord pairs and average independent wall offsets so that short-gap losses have a strictly smaller exponent. -> Full averaged marked-chord mass retained at separated endpoints.
- **step-fixed-end-straight-tubes** [claim](#stmt-lem-pd-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pd-tube): Find fixed-end plateaus using variation and tightness, assemble confined subslab pieces and recover decompositions at unique seam crossings to estimate representation moments. -> Straight-tube fixed-end bridge lower bound.
- **step-widening-tubes-and-bend-sewing** [claim](#stmt-thm-confined-bridge) / [proof](#pm-h-119): Use geometrically growing slabs to open tubes from separated endpoint scale and insert trimmed triangle chords at bends; compare paired cut vectors using localized unique crossings. -> Localized exterior-arm families with controlled mass loss.
- **step-confined-bridge-first-moment-and-normalization** [claim](#stmt-thm-confined-bridge) / [proof](#pm-h-124): Attach disjoint exterior arms to retained marked chords, normalize each arm using only its own port, recover mixed exterior representations for the second moment and sum marked vertices and source ports. Bound the unrestricted upper moment through strip nesting and divide by each bridge mass. -> Confined and unrestricted first moments and their common mean-length exponent.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-finite-pfaffian-evaluation**: The symmetric scalar polynomial has an exact finite Pfaffian definition, with an appended boundary index for odd size. The executable panel checks finite evaluations of this construction; it does not certify the bridge exponents.
- **invariant-small-scalar-polynomials**: The finite evaluation agrees with the explicitly stated small-size scalar polynomials.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-asymptotic-certificate**: Treat agreement of finite scalar-polynomial evaluations as certification of the mean bridge length exponent. -> Finite algebraic evaluations do not establish the analytic asymptotics, localization estimates or sewing bounds.
- **anti-collision-division**: Evaluate the displayed rational Pfaffian expression directly with repeated variables. -> The prefactor divides by variable differences, even though the cleared expression is a polynomial.
- **anti-gas-label-spin-identification**: Interpret the scalar gas labels as the occupied spins of the disk vector. -> This confuses an algebraic representation obtained through scalar characterization with the spin contraction it represents.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The scalar polynomial is explicitly defined by a finite Pfaffian and its cleared numerator is proved polynomial at c2:eq-22. This panel evaluates that finite construction at distinct rational variables. It does not turn the asymptotic bridge, contour or normalization results into finite certificates.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with x, a list of at most 4 distinct rational integers or strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: Rational entries are exact; distinctness avoids prefactor division by zero.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object with rational strings a and b representing a + b*sqrt(2).
# TIME: Finite recursive Pfaffian expansion on the declared bounded input domain; no paper-wide complexity claim.
# SPACE: Finite exact matrix and cached sub-Pfaffians on the declared bounded input domain.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational and quadratic algebraic arithmetic using sympy.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys
from functools import lru_cache
import sympy as sp

payload = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(payload, dict) or set(payload) != {'x'}:
    raise ValueError('expected only x')
raw = payload['x']
if not isinstance(raw, list) or len(raw) > 4:
    raise ValueError('input size outside checked domain')
values = []
for item in raw:
    if isinstance(item, bool) or not isinstance(item, (int, str)):
        raise ValueError('exact rational entry required')
    value = sp.Rational(item)
    values.append(value)
if len(set(values)) != len(values):
    raise ValueError('distinct variables required')
N = len(values)
size = N + N % 2
root = sp.sqrt(2)
d = root
matrix = sp.zeros(size)
prefactor = sp.Integer(1)
for i in range(N):
    for j in range(i + 1, N):
        denominator = values[i]**2 + values[j]**2 + d*values[i]*values[j]
        matrix[i, j] = (values[j]**2 - values[i]**2) / denominator
        matrix[j, i] = -matrix[i, j]
        prefactor *= denominator / (values[j] - values[i])
if N % 2:
    for i in range(N):
        matrix[i, N] = sp.Integer(1)
        matrix[N, i] = -matrix[i, N]

@lru_cache(None)
def pf(indices):
    if not indices:
        return sp.Integer(1)
    total = sp.Integer(0)
    first = indices[0]
    for j in range(1, len(indices)):
        sign = 1 if j % 2 else -1
        remainder = indices[1:j] + indices[j + 1:]
        total += sign * matrix[first, indices[j]] * pf(remainder)
    return total

polynomial = sp.expand(sp.radsimp(sp.cancel(prefactor * pf(tuple(range(size))))))
b = sp.simplify(polynomial.coeff(root))
a = sp.simplify(polynomial - b*root)
if not a.is_Rational or not b.is_Rational:
    raise ArithmeticError('quadratic field reduction failed')
print(json.dumps({'a': str(a), 'b': str(b)}, sort_keys=True))
```

```json
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with x, a list of at most 4 distinct rational integers or strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Rational entries are exact; distinctness avoids prefactor division by zero.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object with rational strings a and b representing a + b*sqrt(2).\n# TIME: Finite recursive Pfaffian expansion on the declared bounded input domain; no paper-wide complexity claim.\n# SPACE: Finite exact matrix and cached sub-Pfaffians on the declared bounded input domain.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational and quadratic algebraic arithmetic using sympy.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom functools import lru_cache\nimport sympy as sp\n\npayload = json.load(sys.stdin)\nif not isinstance(payload, dict) or set(payload) != {'x'}:\n    raise ValueError('expected only x')\nraw = payload['x']\nif not isinstance(raw, list) or len(raw) > 4:\n    raise ValueError('input size outside checked domain')\nvalues = []\nfor item in raw:\n    if isinstance(item, bool) or not isinstance(item, (int, str)):\n        raise ValueError('exact rational entry required')\n    value = sp.Rational(item)\n    values.append(value)\nif len(set(values)) != len(values):\n    raise ValueError('distinct variables required')\nN = len(values)\nsize = N + N % 2\nroot = sp.sqrt(2)\nd = root\nmatrix = sp.zeros(size)\nprefactor = sp.Integer(1)\nfor i in range(N):\n    for j in range(i + 1, N):\n        denominator = values[i]**2 + values[j]**2 + d*values[i]*values[j]\n        matrix[i, j] = (values[j]**2 - values[i]**2) / denominator\n        matrix[j, i] = -matrix[i, j]\n        prefactor *= denominator / (values[j] - values[i])\nif N % 2:\n    for i in range(N):\n        matrix[i, N] = sp.Integer(1)\n        matrix[N, i] = -matrix[i, N]\n\n@lru_cache(None)\ndef pf(indices):\n    if not indices:\n        return sp.Integer(1)\n    total = sp.Integer(0)\n    first = indices[0]\n    for j in range(1, len(indices)):\n        sign = 1 if j % 2 else -1\n        remainder = indices[1:j] + indices[j + 1:]\n        total += sign * matrix[first, indices[j]] * pf(remainder)\n    return total\n\npolynomial = sp.expand(sp.radsimp(sp.cancel(prefactor * pf(tuple(range(size))))))\nb = sp.simplify(polynomial.coeff(root))\na = sp.simplify(polynomial - b*root)\nif not a.is_Rational or not b.is_Rational:\n    raise ArithmeticError('quadratic field reduction failed')\nprint(json.dumps({'a': str(a), 'b': str(b)}, sort_keys=True))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite visit recursion supplies a concrete evaluator: enumerate simple polygons through or enclosing the marked vertex, then recurse on strict interior vertex sets. This computes the spin-weighted visit sum for a finite filled honeycomb domain; it does not compute an asymptotic exponent. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bridge theorem describes a critical ensemble summing all finite lengths and terminal ports. It supplies no game movement rule, trajectory generator, or sampling procedure for a prescribed path length. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The local diagram calculus gives a concrete symbolic identity-checking construction: compose compatible noncrossing diagrams, discard closed-loop contributions, and compare the resulting coefficients for each external connectivity. The paper explicitly performs this comparison for the braid identity. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The scalar-gas identity supplies a finite evaluation construction: enumerate the displayed gas labels at each site, retain assignments satisfying signed neutrality, and sum their specified one-body and pair factors. This evaluates the algebraic pairing for consecutive blocks at generic rapidities. |
| data_structures | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The disk-state construction gives an explicit finite representation by partial noncrossing boundary pairings. Its spin embedding has disjoint support for distinct occupied sets and a triangular independence witness obtained from Dyck words and stack pairing. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The oscillator realization is an analytic representation of fields and traces. Its energy-shell damping estimates justify convergence, but supply no accelerator architecture, arithmetic layout, or hardware execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The inverse pairing is an exact identity for a Cauchy matrix at generic nearby rapidities. It establishes neither computational hardness nor a cryptographic primitive, and only the scalar pairing has the stated removable continuation. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The triangle visit law concerns normalized critical chords conditioned on visiting the inner triangle. Its visit probabilities provide no learning objective, training construction, or guarantee for a learned path model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The straight-tube lemma establishes a positive critical mass of fixed-end bridges under small offset and sufficiently large width. It does not construct an optimal route or provide a numerical threshold for channel dimensioning. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exterior-arm sewing argument controls multiple representations of one lattice path by weighted moments and recoverable cuts. It provides no distributed scheduling, communication, or fault-recovery construction. |
| other (Finite lattice statistical mechanics) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The finite disk transfer construction supplies a normalized fixed vector, and the prescribed spin pairing glues disk states into the cylinder separator observable. At balanced homogeneity this gives a concrete partition-function representation with nonnegative coefficients in polygon fugacity; the asymptotic exponent still requires the analytic proof. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-critical-honeycomb-ensemble** (hypothesis): The path results use the specified honeycomb geometry and critical activity per visited vertex. Extensions to other lattices or off-critical weights are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Transfer the proved exponents to arbitrary graphs or altered path activities.', 'Count terminal half-edges as additional weighted vertices.']
- **limit-all-length-mean** (hypothesis): The mean-length law concerns the critical ensemble summing finite lengths and terminal ports; it does not establish fixed-length displacement statistics. Blocked use: ['Apply the bridge mean as a displacement law for predetermined-length walks.', 'Treat the mean as a bound on every individual path.']
- **limit-endpoint-conditioning** (hypothesis): The annealed arch and triangle conclusions require their stated endpoint summations and conditioning events. Prescribing both endpoints requires a separate result. Blocked use: ['Infer a prescribed dual-endpoint mean directly from the annealed theorem.', 'Remove the inner-triangle conditioning from the bulk visit law.']
- **limit-fixed-corridor-threshold** (nonconstructive): Corridor retention requires a sufficiently large fixed multiplier. A numerical threshold and a finite-scale error tolerance are ABSENT from the stated theorem. Blocked use: ['Choose an exact engineering channel width from the existence assertion.', 'Let the corridor multiplier shrink with the strip scale while retaining the theorem unchanged.']
- **limit-inverse-pairing-collisions** (edge_case): The inverse formula starts at generic distinct rapidities near zero. Its removable continuation concerns the scalar pairing, not the matrix inverse. Blocked use: ['Invert the homogeneous matrix by assuming its inverse extends through collisions.', 'Apply the nearby-rapidity argument at unrestricted parameter values.']
- **limit-disk-interpolation-conventions** (hypothesis): Disk-vector interpolation depends on the prescribed occupancy gauge, degree bounds, descending fusion conditions and compatible half-power sheets. Blocked use: ['Rescale the normalized disk vector freely while retaining its physical coordinates.', 'Apply fusion reductions without checking the rapidity difference and insertion order.']
- **limit-scalar-uniqueness-full-rules** (hypothesis): Scalar uniqueness requires separate symmetry, the polynomial degree bound and all the stated deletion, merging and cluster rules. Same-block fusion alone does not remove the balanced-size ambiguity. Blocked use: ['Claim uniqueness after omitting the cross-block cluster rule.', 'Identify an arbitrary rational family from its base values without verifying the degree bound.']
- **limit-gas-labels-and-poles** (formal_scope): Gas labels are algebraic summation variables distinct from disk spins. Apparent singularities require the source's cancellation or continuation arguments before specialization. Blocked use: ['Sample individual gas terms as an assumed positive physical distribution.', 'Substitute coincident rapidities into uncancelled rational factors.']
- **limit-balanced-physical-pairing** (hypothesis): The positive separator interpretation is asserted at balanced homogeneity. Generic rapidity-dependent pairings remain algebraic objects. Blocked use: ['Apply physical positivity or fugacity monotonicity to arbitrary block sizes and complex rapidities.', 'Identify algebraic gas labels with polygon orientations without the gluing argument.']
- **limit-contour-bound-analytic-regime** (approximation): The homogeneous gas estimate is an asymptotic upper bound uniform in the stated complex neighborhood. It does not provide unrestricted angle control or a finite-size equality. Blocked use: ['Replace the upper bound by equality before normalization and saturation.', 'Interpret finite polynomial evaluations as certification of contour stability or the nome limit.']
- **limit-normalization-not-finite-certificate** (nonconstructive): The homogeneous normalization includes a limiting logarithmic correction derived from projection estimates. A finite Pfaffian evaluation does not decide this asymptotic correction. Blocked use: ['Certify the normalization exponent solely from agreement on small scalar polynomials.', 'Discard the exponential normalization when converting the gas estimate into the physical partition function.']
- **limit-harmonic-saturation-preconditions** (hypothesis): Saturation requires holomorphy, positive real-interval values dominated by the initial value, a locally uniform harmonic upper bound and equality at the other endpoint. Blocked use: ['Propagate equality from a real-axis upper bound without complex-neighborhood control.', 'Apply saturation to signed partition sums lacking the stated positivity and domination.']
- **limit-polygon-shape-and-winding-scope** (formal_scope): The shape estimate concerns translation classes of simple polygons; the winding estimate concerns single simple separators of antipodal off-edge marks. Neither directly bounds unrestricted rooted polygon collections. Blocked use: ['Count every translated or rooted polygon as a separate term while using the translation-class bound unchanged.', 'Replace the single winding-polygon sum by a collection partition function without an additional argument.']
- **limit-filled-domain-versus-positive-excursions** (hypothesis): The recursion and monotonicity require a finite filled domain containing the marked vertex. Comparison with unsigned excursion mass additionally requires a bounded convex domain cut by admissible walls. Blocked use: ['Apply the recursion to a domain with missing enclosed vertices.', 'Assume every individual winding-weighted term has positive real part on an arbitrary filled domain.']
- **limit-separated-endpoints-averaged** (formal_scope): The retained separated-endpoint supply is averaged over independent wall-offset windows. It is not a uniform assertion for every individual triangle. Blocked use: ['Attach disjoint arms using an unproved separated-endpoint supply for one arbitrarily fixed triangle.', 'Discard the wall averaging that suppresses short endpoint gaps.']
- **limit-straight-and-widening-tubes** (hypothesis): Straight-tube supply requires sufficiently large admissible widths and small prescribed lateral offsets. Widening tubes introduce a controlled exponent loss depending on endpoint separation slack. Blocked use: ['Use the tube lower bound for arbitrary endpoint slopes or small widths.', 'Claim widening tubes retain a loss-free finite-scale mass bound.']
- **limit-bend-clearance-and-seam-recovery** (hypothesis): Bent-arm sewing requires separated simple routes, trimmed bend ports and localized seam crossings. Disjointness within one construction alone does not control repeated representations. Blocked use: ['Multiply piece masses for routes whose tube neighborhoods intersect.', 'Count all sewing representations as distinct paths without the paired-cut recovery estimate.']
- **limit-own-port-arm-normalization** (hypothesis): Each arm's thinning factor depends only on its own recovered starting port, role and triangle. This independence is needed when the exterior entry and exit come from different representations. Blocked use: ['Normalize an arm using the other chord endpoint and reuse the mixed-representation second-moment bound.', 'Drop all thinning factors before estimating the recovered exterior arms.']

It suffices to consider $0<\\epsilon<1$; the assertion for larger $\\epsilon$ follows by monotonicity of the retained endpoint set. On the set $|b-a|<H^{1-\\epsilon}$, the weight $G_{ab}=(1+|b-a|)^{-\\tau}$ is at least a constant times $H^{-\\tau(1-\\epsilon)}$. Equation <a href=\"#c2:eq-K3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"c2:eq-K3\">[c2:eq-K3]</a> therefore gives $$\\left\\langle\\sum_{|b-a|<H^{1-\\epsilon}}K_{ab}\\right\\rangle\n \\le H^{z-\\tau\\epsilon+o(1)}.$$ The total average $\\langle\\sum_{a,b}K_{ab}\\rangle$ is $H^{z+o(1)}$, uniformly under the size and depth assumptions above, by <a href=\"#c2:eq-G1\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"c2:eq-G1\">[c2:eq-G1]</a>, <a href=\"#c2:eq-G3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"c2:eq-G3\">[c2:eq-G3]</a>, and the positive cosine comparison. Since $\\tau\\epsilon>0$, the small-gap contribution has a strictly smaller exponent. Hence $$\\left\\langle\\sum_{|b-a|\\ge H^{1-\\epsilon}}K_{ab}\\right\\rangle\n =H^{z+o(1)}\\qquad(\\epsilon>0\\text{ fixed}).\n \\label{c2:eq-separated-supply}$$ The sums are over ordered endpoint pairs, and the average is over the same three independent offset windows as in <a href=\"#c2:eq-K3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"c2:eq-K3\">[c2:eq-K3]</a>.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-237.8"
        }
      ],
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that critical honeycomb bridges crossing a strip of width <i>R</i> have mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$, with the same exponent after confinement to a fixed multiple of the strip width. The initial boundary port is fixed, the terminal port is summed, and all finite lengths receive their critical weights. The bridge mass is comparable to <i>R</i><sup>−1/4</sup>; the half-plane arch kernel at endpoint separation <i>m</i> is comparable to <i>m</i><sup>−5/4</sup>.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

A self-avoiding path crossing a wide strip can make long excursions parallel to its walls. We prove a mean-length exponent of $4/3$ for critical honeycomb walks, both with these excursions unrestricted and with their lateral displacement bounded by a sufficiently large fixed multiple of the strip width. The starting boundary point is fixed, the terminal point is summed over the opposite wall, and all finite lengths receive their critical weights. We also obtain the corresponding mean for large half-plane arches and a bulk visit law for chords in a triangle.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The lattice and the positive path measures

The honeycomb lattice has unit edges in directions $$e_2=(0,-1),\qquad e_0=(\sqrt3/2,1/2),\qquad
 e_1=(-\sqrt3/2,1/2).$$ Put $U=e_0-e_1$ and $V=e_0-e_2$. The two vertex classes are $jU+kV$ and $jU+kV+e_0$, for $j,k\in\mathbb Z$; the three edges from a vertex of the first class have directions $e_0,e_1,e_2$. For $i\in\{0,1,2\}$ and $t\in\frac12+\frac32\mathbb Z$, the line $e_i\cdot y=t$ is an admissible wall. It avoids vertices and cuts edges orthogonally at their midpoints. These midpoints are boundary *ports*. A port-to-port path visits distinct lattice vertices, all strictly inside its prescribed domain. Its two terminal half-edges have no additional weight.

Throughout, a path $\gamma$ with $\ell(\gamma)$ visited vertices has weight $$w(\gamma)=x^{\ell(\gamma)},\qquad
 x=(2\cos(\pi/8))^{-1}=(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}.$$ The connective constant of the honeycomb lattice is $x^{-1}$ by Duminil-Copin and Smirnov <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DuminilCopinSmirnov2012](#ref-DuminilCopinSmirnov2012), Theorem 1]. For a nonempty path between ordinary vertices, vertex count is edge count plus one. All normalizations below use vertex count. We write $f(R)\asymp g(R)$ when their ratio stays between positive constants, and $f(R)=R^{a+o(1)}$ when $\log f(R)/\log R\to a$.

Fix two parallel admissible walls at distance $R\in\frac32\mathbb Z_{>0}$, call them the lower and upper walls, and fix a port $a$ on the lower wall. Let $\tau$ be either unit tangent to the walls and write $\xi(y)=\tau\cdot(y-a)$ for lateral displacement from the source. A *bridge* joins $a$ to any port of the upper wall and otherwise stays strictly between the walls. Write $$B(R)=\sum_{\gamma\text{ bridge}}w(\gamma),\qquad
 M(R)=\sum_{\gamma\text{ bridge}}w(\gamma)\ell(\gamma).$$ The normalized critical bridge law assigns probability $w(\gamma)/B(R)$. For $C>0$, let $B_C(R)$ and $M_C(R)$ be the same sums restricted to paths satisfying $\sup_{y\in\gamma}|\xi(y)|\le CR$, where the supremum includes the terminal ports. The sign chosen for $\tau$ does not affect this restriction.

<a id="stmt-thm-confined-bridge"></a>
<div id="thm:confined-bridge" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Bridge mass retained in a fixed corridor). *There is a constant $C_0<\infty$ such that, for every fixed $C\ge C_0$, $$B_C(R)\asymp B(R)\asymp R^{-1/4},\qquad
 M_C(R)=R^{13/12+o(1)},\qquad M(R)=R^{13/12+o(1)}.$$ Consequently the mean number of visited vertices is $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ both under the unrestricted critical bridge law and under its restriction to this fixed corridor.*

</div>

The constant $C_0$ is independent of the exponent slack implicit in $o(1)$. The theorem concerns a mean under a law that sums all lengths. Its proof will expose why two further distinctions matter: a bulk visit estimate initially counts both arches and bridges, and an endpoint-separation estimate is needed before two disjoint exterior arms can turn those visits into confined bridges.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Boundary and finite-domain consequences

A half-plane *arch* starts at one fixed port and ends at another port of the same wall. Let $h(m)$ be its total critical mass when the terminal port lies $m>0$ port spacings to the right. Let $\mu$ be the unnormalized critical measure with that initial port and the terminal port summed over the wall. Height is distance from the wall; diameter is Euclidean diameter.

<a id="stmt-c2-thm-annealed"></a>
<div id="c2:thm-annealed" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Sharp boundary mass and annealed chord laws). *<span id="thm:boundary" label="thm:boundary"></span>The following conclusions hold as the indicated scale tends to infinity.*

1.  *The arch kernel is nonincreasing and $$h(m)\asymp m^{-5/4},\qquad
     \mu(\operatorname{height}>R)\asymp
     \mu(\operatorname{diameter}>R)\asymp R^{-1/4}.$$*

2.  *For every sufficiently large fixed $C$, normalize $\mu$ either on $R\le\operatorname{diameter}\le CR$, or on $\operatorname{height}\ge R$ and $\operatorname{diameter}\le CR$. Under either law, $\mathbb E\ell=R^{4/3+o(1)}$.*

3.  *Let $$D_R=\{y:e_i\cdot y<R,\ i=0,1,2\},\qquad
     I_R=\{v\text{ a lattice vertex}:e_i\cdot v<(1-\varepsilon)R,
     \ i=0,1,2\},$$ where $R\in\frac12+\frac32\mathbb Z$ and $\varepsilon>0$ is sufficiently small and fixed. Sum critical chord weights over the ordered pair of boundary ports and normalize on chords visiting $I_R$. Uniformly for $v\in I_R$, their visit probability is $R^{-2/3+o(1)}$. The expected total number of visited vertices, and the expected number in $I_R$, are both $R^{4/3+o(1)}$.*

</div>

The endpoints in the last two conclusions are summed. Prescribing both endpoints is a different conditioning problem. The finite bridge reward in Theorem <a href="#thm:confined-bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:confined-bridge">1</a> also supplies an input to renewal arguments for infinite walks; those arguments concern different probability laws and are not needed here.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Historical context and related representations

Nienhuis’s solid-on-solid and Coulomb-gas analysis of the dilute $O(n)$ model gives the two-dimensional polymer exponent predictions at $n=0$ <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Nienhuis1982](#ref-Nienhuis1982)]. Lawler, Schramm and Werner formulated the conjectural $\mathrm{SLE}_{8/3}$ scaling description of planar self-avoiding walk and recovered the predicted displacement exponent $\nu=3/4$ <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LawlerSchrammWerner2004](#ref-LawlerSchrammWerner2004)]. That prediction concerns displacement under a fixed-length law. Our theorem determines a mean length under the critical measure that sums lengths and terminal ports. On the square lattice, Dyhr, Gilbert, Kennedy, Lawler and Passon studied this critical strip ensemble with one fixed endpoint and the other summed, relating it to a conditioned infinite half-plane walk <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[DyhrEtAl2011](#ref-DyhrEtAl2011)].

Duminil-Copin and Smirnov proved the exact honeycomb connective constant using a parafermionic observable <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DuminilCopinSmirnov2012](#ref-DuminilCopinSmirnov2012)]. Their strip argument gives $c/R\le B(R)\le1$ and records the sharper power $R^{-1/4}$ as a prediction. Beaton, Bousquet-Mélou, de Gier, Duminil-Copin and Guttmann later proved $B(R)\to0$ in their study of surface adsorption <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BeatonEtAl2014](#ref-BeatonEtAl2014)]. Glazman and Manolescu gave a shorter proof and a logarithmic subsequence bound, together with boundary two-point-function invariance for a specified class of columnwise rhombic half-plane tilings <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GlazmanManolescu2017](#ref-GlazmanManolescu2017)]. Krachun and Panagiotis obtained a polynomial upper bound on the critical bridge mass and quantitative sub-ballisticity for uniform honeycomb walks <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[KrachunPanagiotis2026](#ref-KrachunPanagiotis2026)].

Our boundary exploration is a nonbacktracking form of the same parafermionic cancellation, with winding phases and positive masses kept distinct. Its relation to integrable rhombic loop weights was developed by Ikhlef and Cardy <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[IkhlefCardy2009](#ref-IkhlefCardy2009)]; Glazman gives criticality and corrected general weight formulas in that setting <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Glazman2015](#ref-Glazman2015)]. The local dilute diagram algebra and its Yang–Baxter parametrization belong to the braid–monoid construction of Grimm and Pearce <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GrimmPearce1993](#ref-GrimmPearce1993)]. Zhou and Batchelor’s periodic transfer analysis supplies the spectral context, including a seam controlling winding-loop weights <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ZhouBatchelor1997](#ref-ZhouBatchelor1997)]. We fix the boundary gauge, caps and scalar factors explicitly here.

Polynomial exchange equations and special-rapidity reductions have counterparts in the dense $O(1)$ sum rule of Di Francesco and Zinn-Justin <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DiFrancescoZinnJustin2005](#ref-DiFrancescoZinnJustin2005)] and the dilute open-boundary $O(1)$ ground states and first fusion sum rule of Garbali and Nienhuis <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[GarbaliNienhuis2017Ground](#ref-GarbaliNienhuis2017Ground), [GarbaliNienhuis2017Sum](#ref-GarbaliNienhuis2017Sum)]. Garbali and Nienhuis also observed that polynomial ground states and the associated exchange and recurrence construction persist at $n=0$ <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GarbaliNienhuis2017Ground](#ref-GarbaliNienhuis2017Ground), Section 4]. Our $n=0$ disk vector, two-site compatibility and scalar normalization are proved below. The reflected current calculation uses the classical level-one lattice realization of Frenkel–Kac and Segal <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[FrenkelKac1980](#ref-FrenkelKac1980), [Segal1981](#ref-Segal1981), [Frenkel1985](#ref-Frenkel1985)]; we give the mode and vacuum-sign calculations required by the physical normalization.

The related polynomial-vacuum construction <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[PolynomialVacuumCompanion](#ref-PolynomialVacuumCompanion), Theorem 1.1] gives the bridge mean exponent and retains it in a corridor whose width is of order $R\log^2R$. The present argument gives sharp boundary mass comparisons and confinement to a fixed multiple of $R$. The two constructions have the same even scalar polynomial and, after a change of lattice scale, the same homogeneous separator partition function (Remark <a href="#rem:pd:scalar-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:pd:scalar-comparison">4</a>). The disk interpolation, normalization estimates and spatial arguments used here are proved directly.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Two exact calculations and the spatial conversion

Our proof uses the dilute-loop representation at loop weight zero. One calculation determines a boundary deficit. On a cylinder with $N$ sites around its circumference, let $h_N(m)$ be the mass of a single upper-half- cylinder arch from a fixed site to the site $m$ spacings to its right, with that oriented boundary interval specifying its lift. We prove $$1-2\cos(3\pi/8)\sum_{m=1}^{N-1}h_N(m)\asymp N^{-1/4}.$$ We first deform the transfer weights by distinct rapidities. Their finite permutation orbit lets us solve the endpoint equations as a matrix-inverse pairing. At coincident rapidities this pairing is comparable to the squared norm of polynomial evaluation against a positive measure. Its asymptotics give the deficit; boundary exploration identities then convert it into height, strip and pointwise endpoint bounds.

The second calculation concerns bulk polygons. Fix two opposite face centers on a cylinder of circumference $2n|U|$, on the same horizontal cut. Give each collection of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple polygons separating the marks the product of weights $2x^{|\Gamma|}$; polygons are unrooted and unoriented, and the empty collection has weight one. We prove that this partition function is $n^{1/6+o(1)}$. A finite disk transfer vector, normalized by polynomial interpolation, gives the partition function as a pairing of two disk vectors. To estimate it, replace the factor two per polygon by $2\cos\theta$. The pairing has a scalar-gas representation. A reflection of its currents gives an upper bound for complex $\theta$, while an independent determinant calculation fixes the scalar normalization. The resulting upper exponent is harmonic in $\theta$. At zero polygon fugacity the partition function equals one. A maximum-principle argument, using this exact value and the monotonicity of the positive polygon sum, forces the upper exponent to be attained also at fugacity two.

To localize this cylinder calculation, we bound translation classes of large polygons and winding polygons on a cylinder. The small polygons around the two marks then contribute two copies of the same planar nesting sum; the remaining polygons cost only a bounded factor. This gives $$Z_R(f)=R^{1/12+o(1)},$$ where $Z_R(f)$ sums nested polygons surrounding a planar face center $f$ and contained in its radius-$R$ ball. An exact finite exploration identity converts this sum into the weight of boundary paths visiting a bulk vertex. It gives the triangle law and, after sewing one exterior arm, the arch mean.

For bridges, both ends need exterior arms. A marked three-leg exploration identity first shows that enough bulk-visit mass has well-separated chord endpoints. Straight tubes with fixed endpoints, widening first tubes and routes with at most two bends supply two disjoint arms. Summing over possible sewing cuts produces many representations of one path; a weighted second-moment estimate controls that multiplicity and recovers the mass of distinct bridges. The resulting bridges stay in one fixed corridor. Summing over order $R^2$ bulk vertices and accounting for order $R$ starting ports gives the raw first-moment exponent $1+1/12=13/12$. The same nesting identity gives the unrestricted upper bound. Division by the bridge mass then yields the mean exponent $4/3$.

The boundary calculation occupies Sections <a href="#c2:boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:boundary">2</a>–<a href="#sec:deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:deficit">4</a>. Sections <a href="#c2:sec-disk-gluing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:sec-disk-gluing">5</a>–<a href="#c2:sec-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:sec-normalization">7</a> prove the cylinder partition estimate. Sections <a href="#c2:two-bridge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:two-bridge">8</a>–<a href="#c2:nesting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:nesting">10</a> pass to planar nesting and bulk visits. Section <a href="#c2:arches" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:arches">11</a> proves the arch law; Sections <a href="#c2:separated" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:separated">12</a> and <a href="#c2:strips" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c2:strips">13</a> establish endpoint separation and complete the confined bridge proof.

<a id="c2:boundary"></a>
### Local exploration and the transfer row

We first determine the critical mass of a half-plane arch reaching height $T$, and the corresponding strip and endpoint masses. The exponent will be $1/4$. The calculation has two inputs: a positive bound from finite boundary exploration, and an exact equation for arcs on a half-cylinder. The latter requires a transfer row whose normalized fixed vector really counts the physical arcs. We construct that vector here and derive its endpoint exchange equations; Section <a href="#sec:orbit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:orbit">3</a> will solve them.

All positive path sums use the critical vertex weight $x=(2\cos(\pi/8))^{-1}$ from the introduction. A path specified by an unordered pair of endpoints is counted once. We use $\lesssim$ for an upper bound up to a scale-independent constant, and $\asymp$ for bounds in both directions.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Finite exploration and a positive arch bound

Write $\mathbf a_{jk}=jU+kV$ and $\mathbf b_{jk}=\mathbf a_{jk}+e_0$. Use the half-plane consisting of the vertices with $k\ge0$, above the bottom wall $e_2\cdot z=1/2$. Let $h(d)$ be the mass of finite paths from $e_2/2$ to $e_2/2+dU$, for $d\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$. A multipath is a collection of paths with disjoint vertices and distinct endpoints.

Put $$s=3/8,\qquad \lambda=\pi/8,\qquad r=\pi s,\qquad c=2\cos r.$$ The following finite exploration is a nonbacktracking form of the honeycomb parafermionic cancellation <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DuminilCopinSmirnov2012](#ref-DuminilCopinSmirnov2012)]. Let $D$ be a bounded convex polygon cut out by the admissible lines $e_\nu\cdot z\in1/2+(3/2)\mathbb Z$. These lines avoid lattice vertices and cut edges orthogonally at their midpoints. Indeed the $e_\nu$-levels of the $\mathbf a$ vertices lie in $(3/2)\mathbb Z$, and increments toward the $\mathbf b$ vertices are $1,-1/2,-1/2$. Only one cut family passes through any port, so no port is a corner.

Start at a port $a$ and explore inward. At each fresh vertex take the two nonbacktracking turns by $\pm\pi/3$, with complex weights $xe^{\pm\mathrm i s\pi/3}$. Their sum is one. Stop at the first exit or the first repeated vertex, before choosing another turn there. Thus a fresh vertex contributes one factor $x$, while the terminal repeated vertex contributes no new factor. There are finitely many histories, and their total complex weight is one.

A repeated-vertex history has a simple stem followed by one traversal of a simple polygon. The stem lies outside that polygon: it begins at the exterior source and cannot cross the polygon before the first repeat. At their attachment the unused third honeycomb edge is therefore exterior. A counterclockwise traversal of the closed polygon has turn $+\pi/3$ at the attachment; entering from the stem and departing around the polygon replaces this by $-\pi/3$. The two possible traversals consequently add turns $+4\pi/3$ and $-4\pi/3$ relative to the incoming stem. Their phase ratio is $e^{\mathrm i s(8\pi/3)}=-1$, so all repeat histories cancel in pairs.

<a id="c2:eq-1"></a>
<a id="c2:eq-2"></a>
For a simple exit path from $a$ to $b$, its turn $W_{ab}$ depends only on the ports. It is the lifted increase of the outward-normal angle along the counterclockwise boundary from $a$ to $b$, minus $\pi$. To see this, close the chord by the complementary counterclockwise boundary arc. The resulting Jordan polygon has total turn $2\pi$ and contributes $+\pi/2$ at each of the two joins. In particular $|W_{ab}|\le\pi$. Writing $h_D(a,b)$ for the positive mass of chords in $D$, we obtain $$\sum_b e^{\mathrm i sW_{ab}}h_D(a,b)=1.
 \label{c2:eq-1}$$ Every summand’s real part is at least its positive mass times $\cos(\pi s)>0$. Exhaust the half-plane by adding cuts $e_\nu\cdot z\le M$, $\nu=0,1$, on the admissible grids. Bottom exits to the right and left have turns $-\pi$ and $+\pi$, respectively. Monotone convergence of their positive masses and reflection symmetry give $$h(d)=h(-d),\qquad c\sum_{d>0}h(d)\le1.
 \label{c2:eq-2}$$ This bound will control every component of the finite-cylinder transfer vector without assuming the boundary exponent that we seek.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The local diagram weights and braid identity

We use the trigonometric dilute-loop weights in <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[GlazmanManolescu2017](#ref-GlazmanManolescu2017)]. The normalized coefficients below also follow by dividing the weights in <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[ZhouBatchelor1997](#ref-ZhouBatchelor1997), Eq. (1.2)] by their empty-tile weight. Their diagram algebra is the braid–monoid calculus of Grimm and Pearce <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GrimmPearce1993](#ref-GrimmPearce1993), Eq. (4.20)]. Boundary contractions depend on the gauge. We therefore fix the coefficients explicitly and verify the braid identity in this normalization. This identity will let an adjacent column exchange pass through the transfer row.

Put $S=3\lambda,\ a_0=2\lambda,\ b=\sin(2\lambda)=\cos(2\lambda),\ d=\sin(3\lambda)=\cos\lambda$, and $$P_u=\sin u,\quad Q_u=\sin(S-u),\quad I_u=\sin(a_0-u),\quad J_u=\sin(u-\lambda).$$ Make a two-strand braid box $\check R(u)$ with ports in order input left, input right, output right, output left around the box. Use weights below, all divided by $T_u$: $$\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{configuration}&\text{numerator}\\ \hline
\emptyset &T_u=bd+P_uQ_u=\sin(2\lambda+u)\sin(3\lambda+u)\\
\text{one strand at the same position in/out}& A_u=bQ_u\\
\text{one input cap or one output cup}& B_u=bP_u\\
\text{one strand changing position}& C_u=P_uQ_u\\
\text{both through at same positions}& D_u=I_u Q_u\\
\text{cap and cup}& E_u=J_u P_u .
\end{array}$$ Figure <a href="#fig:box-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:box-order">1</a>(a) fixes the cyclic order used by the table. A rotation of a box rotates its ports and connectivities together.

Only noncrossing diagrams are used. In compositions sum linearly over compatible diagrams (occupancies must match at glued ports, and strands concatenate), discarding terms with any closed loop. An untouched line has the vacant identity term and the single through-strand identity term.

<a id="c2:eq-3"></a>
In this calculus the boxes satisfy the additive braid identity $$\check R_1(v)\check R_2(u+v)\check R_1(u)=
 \check R_2(u)\check R_1(u+v)\check R_2(v),                              \label{c2:eq-3}$$ where the subscripts are first and second neighboring pairs among three lines. Here is a verification using unnormalized weights (the denominators are common). Under $u\mathrel{\pdfliteral page{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}\mapsto\pdfliteral page{EMC}}S-u$ the box turns a quarter turn in the diagram sense, i.e. $A,B$ exchange and $D,E$ exchange (other weights unchanged).

In the three boxes of the left side of <a href="#c2:eq-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="c2:eq-3">[c2:eq-3]</a> the six external ports in order (three inputs left to right, then three outputs right to left) belong in consecutive pairs to the first, middle and last crossings (input first ordering). The remaining ports form the internal triangle (each box connects to the next and the previous). Thus write the external pairs as $L_i,R_i;\ L_j,R_j;\ L_k,R_k$ in cyclic order, with box parameters $i,j,k$ respectively, satisfying $i+j+k=S$ (these parameters are $u,S-u-v,v$; the middle box has used the quarter turn). In each box $B$ pairs the two exterior (or two interior) ports, and $A$ connects an exterior port to the nearest interior port ($R$ towards the next tile, $L$ towards the previous); $D$ gives both latter connections, $E$ gives both caps, $C$ gives one opposite connection.

For the right side of <a href="#c2:eq-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="c2:eq-3">[c2:eq-3]</a> the whole placement, with the parameters, shifts by three external ports (the $u$-box is now last at outputs three and two, and the $v$-box first at inputs two and three). We just need invariance of all connectivity weights under this inversion of the boundary data.

Figure <a href="#fig:box-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:box-order">1</a>(b) records this triangle incidence and the boundary inversion. The picture allows the connectivity comparison to be made independently of geometric rhombus angles.

<figure id="fig:box-order">

<figcaption>(a) The four ports of a braid box, in cyclic order. (b) After rotating the middle box in the three-box product, the exterior ports occur in the numbered cyclic order shown. An edge between boxes is a glued internal edge. The opposite product uses the boundary data shifted by three positions, as indicated. The picture records incidence and cyclic order, rather than geometric rhombus angles.</figcaption>
</figure>

Labeled underneath as "(a) A single box". (b) Three boxes arranged around a central triangular opening, with box parameters $i$ (top), $j$ (bottom-left), and $k$ (bottom-right). Six exterior ports with outward arrows are shown: $R_i\ (2)$ and $L_i\ (1)$ at the top box; $L_j\ (3)$ and $R_j\ (4)$ at the bottom-left box; $L_k\ (5)$ and $R_k\ (6)$ at the bottom-right box. Here is the nontrivial scalar list for this comparison (up to cyclic triangle symmetry, reflection and reversing the comparison): $$\begin{array}{l|ll}
 &\text{weight}&\text{after inversion}\\\hline
 2\text{ ports}, L_i,R_i
     &B_iT_jT_k+D_iB_jB_k&T_i A_j A_k+B_i C_j C_k\\
 2,\ L_i,L_j
     &C_i A_j T_k+A_i C_j B_k&A_j C_k T_i+C_j A_k B_i\\
 4,\ L_i,R_i\text{ absent}
     &T_i B_jB_k+B_i(D_jE_k+E_jD_k)& D_i A_j A_k\\
     &B_i D_jD_k & B_i C_j C_k+E_i A_j A_k\\
 4,\ L_i,L_j\text{ absent}
     & A_i C_j B_k+C_i A_j E_k & A_j C_k D_i\\
 6   &B_iB_jB_k+\sum_{\rm cyc} D_i E_j E_k &D_iD_jD_k .
\end{array}$$ For example, in the first line only $L_i,R_i$ are occupied. They can pair directly in box $i$, leaving boxes $j,k$ empty, with weight $B_iT_jT_k$. Otherwise $D_i$ sends both endpoints into the triangle, and caps in $j,k$ complete the route, with weight $D_iB_jB_k$. After inversion the active endpoints are $R_j,L_k$. Their route through the $j$–$k$ edge has weight $T_iA_jA_k$; the route through box $i$ has weight $B_iC_jC_k$.

Indeed internal occupancies have even parity combined with external ones at each box. For all exterior counts even per box, this means no internal strands or else all three internal edges active (a closed internal triangle contributes zero); for two odd counts they are linked by either of the two paths in the triangle, with the two possible pairings at any box having degree four.

For four ports with $L_i,R_i$ absent the first line for that case pairs the exterior pairs within $j,k$, the second exchanges. With $L_i,L_j$ absent the displayed line pairs $R_i,R_j$ to each other and $L_k,R_k$ to each other; the other equality just switches $k,i$. The six-port comparison shown pairs within each exterior pair versus pairing across the gaps. Cases with only two opposite ports occupied or only two opposite absent have matchings themselves invariant under inversion, as do the three remaining matchings in the six-port case (those with one within-box exterior pair and one opposite pair). Together with the empty exterior this exhausts the cases: a two-site subset (occupied or absent) is at separation one, two or three along the hexagon.

For clarity the two columns agree by the following identities. $Q_jQ_k=P_jP_k+dP_i$, $C_j+C_k=C_i+2\cos i\,P_jP_k$, $I_j I_k-P_jP_k=b J_i$, $I_i Q_k-P_i J_k=b P_j$ by angle sums. The difference in line one, divided by $b^2$, reduces to $P_jP_k[D_i-bd+P_i\sin(S+i)]$, zero ($D_i=T_{-i}$).

In line two use $T=bd+C,\ C_i-C_k=P_j\sin(i-k)$, $Q_iP_k-Q_kP_i=d\sin(k-i)$. In line four cancel $bP_i Q_jQ_k$ to use $I_jI_k=P_jP_k+bJ_i$, and in line five cancel $b Q_i Q_j P_k$ to use $I_iQ_k=bP_j+P_iJ_k$; as throughout one can check generic parameters and then continue.
